HCA 13/125: PERSONAL ANSWERS, HIGH COURT OF ADMIRALTY, 1652 TO 1654
UNEDITED MACHINE TRANSCRIPTION (RAW), Version 1.3, 31 October 2026
MarineLives / Colin Greenstreet

This front matter was drafted by Claude Fable 5.1 (medium) in Claude Code, and read, edited and approved by Colin Greenstreet on 31 October 2026 at 15:45.

Who did what. The transcription itself was made by Claude Opus 5.5 (Anthropic) through the Claude API, one request per page, as described in section 3. The pipeline around it (the script, the system prompt, the run, the checks and this front matter) was built and run by Claude Fable 5.1 (medium) working in Claude Code on Colin Greenstreet's PC on 31 October 2026, under his instruction and with his approval at each step. Colin Greenstreet photographed the volume, set the design ("describe, do not identify") and decided on publication. No human has read the transcription.

Contents: 1. What this file is. 2. Provenance of the images. 3. How the transcription was made, and when. 4. Personal answers and depositions. 5. How to cite, and contact. Appendix A: the system prompt. Appendix B: the script.

1. What this file is

This is a first, unedited machine transcription of the whole of HCA 13/125, a bound book of personal answers from the English High Court of Admiralty held at The National Archives, Kew. It is published so that it can be searched. No page has been checked by a human. It is a finding aid, not a text: use it to locate a page, then read the photograph. The transcriptions of HCA 13/20 to HCA 13/79 elsewhere in this repository are edited texts and stand on a different footing.

2. Provenance of the images

Colin Greenstreet photographed the volume at The National Archives on 14 May 2015 with a Canon PowerShot SX510 HS. The camera produced 416 files, IMG_8476.JPG to IMG_8892.JPG (the camera skipped number 8618), each of one page or opening, 2664 by 4000 pixels. The MarineLives wiki names these images IMG_115_05_8476 to IMG_115_05_8892, and that is the name used for each page below. The volume is not foliated, so the image number stands in place of folio and side. The first two images are the cover and the last is a blank. Images from IMG_8894 onward belong to HCA 13/126 and are not in this file.

The images are not on the MarineLives wiki, apart from a handful of pages uploaded in 2015. They are available on request from Colin Greenstreet (contact in section 5).

3. How the transcription was made, and when

Each image was rotated upright and reduced to 1,568 pixels on its long edge (1,044 by 1,568 pixels, about 15 per cent of the camera's pixels), which is the largest size the Claude API accepts before it reduces an image itself. Each was sent on 31 October 2026 as one request to Claude Opus 5.5 through the Message Batches API: 416 requests, submitted at 14:51 and returned at 14:55 UK time, 1,368,640 input tokens and 873,082 output tokens, at a cost of $11.47 at batch rates. The model ran with adaptive thinking at effort "high", an output limit of 8,000 tokens a page, and a fixed system prompt, the "Recursive Archivist", given in full in Appendix A. The prompt told the model to describe the page and not to identify anyone: to transcribe diplomatically, keeping original spelling, abbreviations and line breaks; never to complete a name from context, from the cause title or from history; and to return, with the transcription, a short structured description of each page (page kind, date as written, document type, parties as written, ships, signature or mark, legibility). Those descriptions are kept by MarineLives in a finding-aid table and are not in this file. The script that prepared the images, submitted the batch and assembled this file is given in Appendix B.

A test of ten pages preceded the full run. On the one page that the wiki had already transcribed by hand (IMG_115_05_8699, the answer of Otto George, 29 March 1653), the machine agreed with the hand transcription on most of the text, kept abbreviations the 2015 transcriber had expanded, differed from it in ten words, and left one surname unread rather than guess it. No other page has been compared with a human reading.

What to expect. The model may misread letters (B for C in a surname has been seen), may silently normalise a spelling, may mis-segment a line, and will write [illegible] where a hand transcriber might have risked a reading. Names in particular are as the machine read them. Where the model was unsure it wrote [?] after the word.

Marks used: [?] doubtful word; [illegible] unread stretch; ~~word~~ deletion; \word/ interlineation; [margin] marginal note, given on its own line before the text it stands beside.

Page headings: "HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_nnnn" on its own line before each page, followed by one bracketed line of the machine's page description, then the transcribed lines in reading order, then a blank line.

4. Personal answers and depositions

The volumes HCA 13/20 to HCA 13/79 in this repository are examination books. They hold depositions: the sworn statements of witnesses, examined on the articles of an allegation or on interrogatories, written down by a clerk in the third person ("hee saith and deposeth"). The witness is usually not a party to the suit.

A personal answer is the other side of the same procedure. It is the sworn reply of a party, normally the defendant, to the allegation or articles brought against him, made in his own person before the judge or a surrogate. The answer was drawn up in advance, usually by the party's proctor, read over, sworn to and signed, which is why so many pages here end in a signature or a mark, and why a marginal note such as "Repeated before Dr Exton one of the Judges" records the swearing. The text runs in the first person at one remove ("To the said allegation he answereth that ..."). The National Archives catalogues the HCA 13 series as examinations and answers; the personal answers were kept in their own books, of which HCA 13/125 is one, and the dates read by the machine in this volume run from the middle of 1652 to the beginning of 1654, the years of the first Anglo-Dutch war. Bound in among the answers are a few exhibits, read by the machine as charter parties and accounts. [This paragraph was drafted by Claude Fable 5.1 from the contents of the run and from general knowledge of Admiralty procedure, and checked by Colin Greenstreet before publication; corrections welcome.]

5. How to cite, and contact

HCA 13/125, IMG_115_05_nnnn, unedited machine transcription (Claude Opus 5.5, 31 October 2026), MarineLives / Colin Greenstreet, from this repository; please cite the image number, and treat any quotation as unverified until read against the photograph. The file is made available on the same open basis as the other transcriptions in this repository. Questions, corrections and requests for images: on application to Colin Greenstreet through his LinkedIn account, https://www.linkedin.com/in/colin-greenstreet-7434b9/.

Appendix A. The system prompt sent with every page

You are the Recursive Archivist, a cataloguing assistant working through one photograph at a time of a bound
volume from the English High Court of Admiralty, HCA 13/125, a book of personal answers (defendants' sworn written answers to
allegations) of the early 1650s. Your job is to DESCRIBE what is on this one page, in the order it appears, and to transcribe it.
You do NOT identify people, and you do NOT infer anything from outside this image.

Rules
1. Work from this image only. Never join a name on this page to a name you may expect from another page; never guess a name
   from context, from the cause title, or from general historical knowledge. If a name is partly illegible, transcribe the
   legible letters and mark the rest [?] or [illegible]. Do not complete it.
2. Diplomatic transcription: original spelling, capitalisation and punctuation; keep abbreviations as written (e.g. "sd", "wch",
   "ye", "Comp:"), do not expand or modernise; keep u/v and i/j as written; mark doubtful words with [?] after the word;
   use [illegible] for a stretch you cannot read; mark a deletion as ~~text~~ and an interlineation as \text/; keep the
   scribe's line breaks, one line per array element, in reading order; marginal notes go in their own lines prefixed "[margin] ".
3. If the page is not a text page (blank, cover, flyleaf, slip of paper, ruler or colour card, finger, out of focus beyond
   reading), say so in page_kind and keep the transcription empty.
4. Dates: give the date exactly as written, then a normalised ISO-style date. The English civil year began on 25 March, so a
   date between 1 January and 24 March carries both years in old_style_note (e.g. "Old Style; 29 January 1652 as written
   = 29 January 1653 by modern reckoning, i.e. 1652/3"). If no date is on the page, leave both empty; do not borrow a date.
5. Confidence: for each structured field give a number 0-1 that is your own estimate; 1 means plainly legible and unambiguous.
6. Output a single JSON object and nothing else, no code fences, with exactly these keys:
   image (string, the image number given to you), page_kind (one of: text page, blank, cover, flyleaf, slip, unreadable, other),
   date_as_written, date_normalised, old_style_note,
   document_type (one of: personal answer, deposition, inventory, account, charter party, interrogatory, sentence, allegation,
   other, uncertain), language (e.g. English, Latin, English with Latin formulae),
   cause_or_parties_as_written (the cause title or parties exactly as written, else empty),
   ships (array of ship names exactly as written, may be empty),
   signoff_present (one of: signature, mark, none, unclear), signoff_name_as_written (string, may be empty),
   legibility (integer 1-5, 5 = clear), page_notes (short free text on layout, hands, damage, continuation marks; no identification),
   transcription (array of strings, one per manuscript line),
   confidence (object with a 0-1 number for each of: page_kind, date, document_type, language, cause_or_parties, ships, signoff,
   transcription).
Describe, do not identify.

Appendix B. The script (Python 3.14, anthropic SDK 1.15.0, Pillow 12.1.1)

The script has six steps: download (read the source images and log a checksum of each), prepare (rotate and reduce), submit (one batch request per image, keyed by the wiki image name), status, collect (save every raw reply, parse the JSON, log any refusal or truncation, sum the token usage) and outputs (the finding-aid table and this file's page text). Version 0.2 differs from the version that ran only in the parser, which learned to accept the interlineation mark.

# Recursive Archivist pilot, HCA 13/125 (personal answers, 1652-53), v0.2, 31 October 2026.
# v0.2 (15:00): parse_json repairs invalid JSON escapes (the prompt's interlineation mark, backslash-text-slash, made five replies unparseable in
# the full run); nothing else changed, so results need no re-run. v0.1 is in archive/.
# Written by Claude Fable 5.1 (High) for Colin Greenstreet; design agreed in chat 31 Oct 2026 (Colin's term "Recursive Archivist").
# Purpose: one Claude Message-Batches request per camera image; each returns structured JSON (page kind, date, document type,
# parties, ships, signoff, legibility) plus a line-by-line diplomatic transcription. Describe, do not identify: the model is told
# never to join names across pages or guess names from context. Outputs: a finding-aid TSV, one RAW text file of the volume
# in image order, a run record. Nothing here touches the wiki or GitHub.
# Structure: subcommands  download | prepare | submit | status | collect | outputs   (see USAGE below).
# Weaknesses (stated up front): the model's reading is a machine reading that may silently normalise spelling; JSON is asked for
# in the prompt, not enforced by a schema, so a reply that is not valid JSON is kept raw and flagged; the server-side fallback
# parameter is REJECTED on the Batches API, so refusals are logged, not retried (see run record).
# Settings of record: model claude-opus-5-5, effort high, adaptive thinking (no thinking parameter), max_tokens 8000,
# image long edge 1568 px (JPEG q90), EXIF-rotated before sending. Key from ANTHROPIC_API_KEY only; never printed.
import os, sys, json, time, base64, hashlib, csv, re, datetime, pathlib

IMG_DIR   = pathlib.Path(r"C:\Users\colin\OneDrive\Desktop\TNA_Image_Files\115_05")
HERE      = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parent
PREP_DIR  = HERE / "prepared-1568"
RES_DIR   = HERE / "results"
LOG_DIR   = HERE / "logs"
FIRST, LAST, ABSENT = 8476, 8892, {8618, 8893}
MODEL, MAX_TOKENS, EFFORT, LONG_EDGE = "claude-opus-5-5", 8000, "high", 1568
VOLUME = "HCA 13/125"

SYSTEM_PROMPT = """You are the Recursive Archivist, a cataloguing assistant working through one photograph at a time of a bound
volume from the English High Court of Admiralty, HCA 13/125, a book of personal answers (defendants' sworn written answers to
allegations) of the early 1650s. Your job is to DESCRIBE what is on this one page, in the order it appears, and to transcribe it.
You do NOT identify people, and you do NOT infer anything from outside this image.

Rules
1. Work from this image only. Never join a name on this page to a name you may expect from another page; never guess a name
   from context, from the cause title, or from general historical knowledge. If a name is partly illegible, transcribe the
   legible letters and mark the rest [?] or [illegible]. Do not complete it.
2. Diplomatic transcription: original spelling, capitalisation and punctuation; keep abbreviations as written (e.g. "sd", "wch",
   "ye", "Comp:"), do not expand or modernise; keep u/v and i/j as written; mark doubtful words with [?] after the word;
   use [illegible] for a stretch you cannot read; mark a deletion as ~~text~~ and an interlineation as \\text/; keep the
   scribe's line breaks, one line per array element, in reading order; marginal notes go in their own lines prefixed "[margin] ".
3. If the page is not a text page (blank, cover, flyleaf, slip of paper, ruler or colour card, finger, out of focus beyond
   reading), say so in page_kind and keep the transcription empty.
4. Dates: give the date exactly as written, then a normalised ISO-style date. The English civil year began on 25 March, so a
   date between 1 January and 24 March carries both years in old_style_note (e.g. "Old Style; 29 January 1652 as written
   = 29 January 1653 by modern reckoning, i.e. 1652/3"). If no date is on the page, leave both empty; do not borrow a date.
5. Confidence: for each structured field give a number 0-1 that is your own estimate; 1 means plainly legible and unambiguous.
6. Output a single JSON object and nothing else, no code fences, with exactly these keys:
   image (string, the image number given to you), page_kind (one of: text page, blank, cover, flyleaf, slip, unreadable, other),
   date_as_written, date_normalised, old_style_note,
   document_type (one of: personal answer, deposition, inventory, account, charter party, interrogatory, sentence, allegation,
   other, uncertain), language (e.g. English, Latin, English with Latin formulae),
   cause_or_parties_as_written (the cause title or parties exactly as written, else empty),
   ships (array of ship names exactly as written, may be empty),
   signoff_present (one of: signature, mark, none, unclear), signoff_name_as_written (string, may be empty),
   legibility (integer 1-5, 5 = clear), page_notes (short free text on layout, hands, damage, continuation marks; no identification),
   transcription (array of strings, one per manuscript line),
   confidence (object with a 0-1 number for each of: page_kind, date, document_type, language, cause_or_parties, ships, signoff,
   transcription).
Describe, do not identify."""


def numbers():
    return [n for n in range(FIRST, LAST + 1) if n not in ABSENT]


def wiki_name(n): return f"IMG_115_05_{n}"
def src_path(n):  return IMG_DIR / f"IMG_{n}.JPG"
def prep_path(n): return PREP_DIR / f"{wiki_name(n)}_1568.jpg"
def now():        return datetime.datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S")


def log(name, line):
    LOG_DIR.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    with open(LOG_DIR / name, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write(f"{now()}\t{line}\n")


# ---------- download: hydrate OneDrive online-only files by reading them; log every failure ----------
def cmd_download(nums, batch=50, pause=2.0):
    ok, bad = 0, []
    for i, n in enumerate(nums, 1):
        p = src_path(n)
        try:
            with open(p, "rb") as f:
                h = hashlib.sha256(); size = 0
                for chunk in iter(lambda: f.read(1 << 20), b""):
                    h.update(chunk); size += len(chunk)
            log("download_log.tsv", f"{n}\tok\t{size}\t{h.hexdigest()}"); ok += 1
        except Exception as e:
            log("download_log.tsv", f"{n}\tFAIL\t\t{type(e).__name__}: {e}"); bad.append(n)
        if i % batch == 0:
            print(f"{now()} {i}/{len(nums)} read, {len(bad)} failures so far", flush=True); time.sleep(pause)
    print(f"download done: {ok} ok, {len(bad)} failed: {bad}")


# ---------- prepare: EXIF-rotate, resize to 1568 px long edge, save JPEG; record sha256 ----------
def cmd_prepare(nums):
    from PIL import Image, ImageOps
    PREP_DIR.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    for n in nums:
        out = prep_path(n)
        if out.exists():
            continue
        try:
            im = Image.open(src_path(n)); im = ImageOps.exif_transpose(im)
            w, h = im.size; s = LONG_EDGE / max(w, h)
            if s < 1:
                im = im.resize((round(w * s), round(h * s)), Image.LANCZOS)
            im.convert("RGB").save(out, "JPEG", quality=90, optimize=True)
            log("prepare_log.tsv", f"{n}\tok\t{w}x{h}->{im.size[0]}x{im.size[1]}\t{out.stat().st_size}\t{hashlib.sha256(out.read_bytes()).hexdigest()}")
        except Exception as e:
            log("prepare_log.tsv", f"{n}\tFAIL\t{type(e).__name__}: {e}")
    print(f"prepare done: {sum(prep_path(n).exists() for n in nums)}/{len(nums)} prepared")


# ---------- submit: one request per image, custom_id = wiki name ----------
def build_request(n):
    from anthropic.types.message_create_params import MessageCreateParamsNonStreaming
    from anthropic.types.messages.batch_create_params import Request
    data = base64.standard_b64encode(prep_path(n).read_bytes()).decode()
    return Request(custom_id=wiki_name(n), params=MessageCreateParamsNonStreaming(
        model=MODEL, max_tokens=MAX_TOKENS, output_config={"effort": EFFORT},
        system=SYSTEM_PROMPT,
        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": [
            {"type": "image", "source": {"type": "base64", "media_type": "image/jpeg", "data": data}},
            {"type": "text", "text": f"Image number: {wiki_name(n)} (camera file IMG_{n}.JPG, {VOLUME}). "
                                     f"Describe and transcribe this one page as instructed. Return the JSON object only."}]}]))


def cmd_submit(nums, tag):
    import anthropic
    if not os.environ.get("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"):
        sys.exit("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY not in environment; stopping (the key is never asked for in chat).")
    missing = [n for n in nums if not prep_path(n).exists()]
    if missing:
        sys.exit(f"not prepared: {missing}")
    client = anthropic.Anthropic()
    reqs = [build_request(n) for n in nums]
    b = client.messages.batches.create(requests=reqs)
    manifest = {"tag": tag, "batch_id": b.id, "created": now(), "model": MODEL, "max_tokens": MAX_TOKENS, "effort": EFFORT,
                "thinking": "adaptive (parameter omitted)", "fallbacks": "not sent: parameter rejected on the Batches API",
                "long_edge_px": LONG_EDGE, "images": [wiki_name(n) for n in nums],
                "system_prompt_sha256": hashlib.sha256(SYSTEM_PROMPT.encode()).hexdigest()}
    RES_DIR.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    (RES_DIR / f"manifest_{tag}.json").write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=1), encoding="utf-8")
    print(f"submitted batch {b.id} with {len(reqs)} requests; status {b.processing_status}")


def cmd_status(tag):
    import anthropic
    m = json.loads((RES_DIR / f"manifest_{tag}.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    b = anthropic.Anthropic().messages.batches.retrieve(m["batch_id"])
    print(f"{now()} {b.id} {b.processing_status} counts={b.request_counts}")
    return b.processing_status


# ---------- collect: save every raw result, parse JSON, log refusals / truncations / errors, sum usage ----------
def repair_escapes(t):
    # a backslash not followed by a legal JSON escape (the scribe's interlineation mark \word/) becomes a literal backslash
    def fix(m):
        run, c = m.group(1), m.group(2)
        if len(run) % 2 == 0 or c in '"\\/bfnrtu':   # even run = already-escaped backslashes; odd + legal escape = fine
            return run + c
        return run + "\\" + c                          # odd run + other char: make the last backslash literal
    return re.sub(r'(\\+)(.)', fix, t, flags=re.S)


def parse_json(text):
    t = text.strip()
    t = re.sub(r"^```(?:json)?\s*|\s*```$", "", t)
    try:
        return json.loads(t), None
    except Exception:
        pass
    t = repair_escapes(t)
    try:
        return json.loads(t), None
    except Exception as e:
        i, j = t.find("{"), t.rfind("}")
        if i >= 0 and j > i:
            try:
                return json.loads(t[i:j + 1]), None
            except Exception as e2:
                return None, f"{e2}"
        return None, f"{e}"


def cmd_collect(tag):
    import anthropic
    m = json.loads((RES_DIR / f"manifest_{tag}.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    client = anthropic.Anthropic()
    out_dir = RES_DIR / tag; out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    usage = {"input_tokens": 0, "output_tokens": 0, "cache_read_input_tokens": 0, "cache_creation_input_tokens": 0}
    rows, refusals, truncated, errored, unparsed = {}, [], [], [], []
    for r in client.messages.batches.results(m["batch_id"]):
        cid = r.custom_id
        (out_dir / f"{cid}.raw.json").write_text(r.model_dump_json(indent=1), encoding="utf-8")
        if r.result.type != "succeeded":
            errored.append((cid, r.result.type, getattr(getattr(r.result, "error", None), "type", ""))); continue
        msg = r.result.message
        for k in usage:
            usage[k] += getattr(msg.usage, k, 0) or 0
        if msg.stop_reason == "refusal":
            sd = msg.stop_details
            refusals.append((cid, getattr(sd, "category", None), getattr(sd, "explanation", None))); continue
        text = "".join(b.text for b in msg.content if b.type == "text")
        if msg.stop_reason == "max_tokens":
            truncated.append(cid)
        data, err = parse_json(text)
        if data is None:
            unparsed.append((cid, err)); data = {"image": cid, "page_kind": "PARSE ERROR", "transcription": text.splitlines()}
        data["_stop_reason"] = msg.stop_reason; data["_output_tokens"] = msg.usage.output_tokens
        data["_input_tokens"] = msg.usage.input_tokens; data["_model"] = msg.model
        rows[cid] = data
    (out_dir / "parsed.json").write_text(json.dumps(rows, indent=1, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
    cost = (usage["input_tokens"] * 2 + usage["output_tokens"] * 10 + usage["cache_read_input_tokens"] * 0.2
            + usage["cache_creation_input_tokens"] * 2.5) / 1e6
    summary = {"tag": tag, "batch_id": m["batch_id"], "collected": now(), "succeeded": len(rows) + len(refusals),
               "errored": errored, "refusals": refusals, "truncated_max_tokens": truncated, "unparsed_json": unparsed,
               "usage": usage, "cost_usd_batch_rates": round(cost, 4),
               "price_basis": "Opus 5.5 batch: $2 in / $10 out per MTok; cache read $0.20, cache write $2.50 per MTok; list price would be double"}
    (out_dir / "summary.json").write_text(json.dumps(summary, indent=1, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
    print(json.dumps(summary, indent=1, ensure_ascii=False))


# ---------- outputs: finding-aid TSV and RAW volume text ----------
FIELDS = ["image", "camera_file", "page_kind", "date_as_written", "date_normalised", "old_style_note", "document_type", "language",
          "cause_or_parties_as_written", "ships", "signoff_present", "signoff_name_as_written", "legibility", "page_notes", "lines",
          "conf_page_kind", "conf_date", "conf_document_type", "conf_language", "conf_cause_or_parties", "conf_ships", "conf_signoff",
          "conf_transcription", "stop_reason", "output_tokens"]
CONF_KEYS = ["page_kind", "date", "document_type", "language", "cause_or_parties", "ships", "signoff", "transcription"]


def cmd_outputs(tag, version, datestamp):
    rows = json.loads((RES_DIR / tag / "parsed.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    order = [wiki_name(n) for n in numbers() if wiki_name(n) in rows]
    clean = lambda v: re.sub(r"\s+", " ", str(v if v is not None else "")).strip()
    today = datetime.date.today().strftime("%d %B %Y")
    tsv = HERE / f"HCA13-125_Finding_Aid_{tag}_v{version}_{datestamp}.tsv"
    with open(tsv, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as f:
        w = csv.writer(f, delimiter="\t", lineterminator="\n"); w.writerow(FIELDS)
        for cid in order:
            d = rows[cid]; c = d.get("confidence") or {}; tr = d.get("transcription") or []
            ships = d.get("ships"); ships = "; ".join(map(str, ships)) if isinstance(ships, list) else ships
            w.writerow([cid, f"IMG_{cid[-4:]}.JPG", clean(d.get("page_kind")), clean(d.get("date_as_written")),
                        clean(d.get("date_normalised")), clean(d.get("old_style_note")), clean(d.get("document_type")),
                        clean(d.get("language")), clean(d.get("cause_or_parties_as_written")), clean(ships),
                        clean(d.get("signoff_present")), clean(d.get("signoff_name_as_written")), clean(d.get("legibility")),
                        clean(d.get("page_notes")), len(tr)] + [clean(c.get(k)) for k in CONF_KEYS]
                       + [clean(d.get("_stop_reason")), clean(d.get("_output_tokens"))])
    raw = HERE / f"HCA_13_125_Full_Volume_Machine_Transcription_RAW_Ver.{version}_{datestamp}.txt"
    hdr = (
        "HCA 13/125 - UNEDITED MACHINE TRANSCRIPTION - RAW\n"
        "Volume: The National Archives, HCA 13/125, High Court of Admiralty, personal answers, 1652-1653 (unfoliated; pages keyed by camera image number)\n"
        f"Transcription: Claude Opus 5.5 (Anthropic), effort high, adaptive thinking, Message Batches API, one request per image, {today}\n"
        f"Run tag: {tag}; pages in this file: {len(order)} of {len(numbers())} images\n"
        "Images: photographs by Colin Greenstreet at The National Archives, Kew, 14 May 2015 (Canon PowerShot SX510 HS), camera files IMG_8476.JPG to IMG_8892.JPG,\n"
        "  sent to the model EXIF-rotated and reduced to 1568 px on the long edge; wiki image name IMG_115_05_nnnn = camera file IMG_nnnn.JPG\n"
        "WARNING: no page of this text has been checked by a human. It is a machine reading that may misread, omit or silently normalise\n"
        "  seventeenth-century spelling, and may mis-segment lines. Names are as the machine read them and must not be relied on without the image.\n"
        "  Use it to find pages, then read the photograph. Marks: [?] doubtful word, [illegible] unread stretch, ~~x~~ deletion, \\x/ interlineation, [margin] marginal note.\n"
        "Page heading format: \"HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_nnnn\" on its own line before each page (the volume is unfoliated, so the image number replaces folio and side).\n"
        f"Cite as: HCA 13/125, IMG_115_05_nnnn, machine transcription (Claude Opus 5.5, {today}), MarineLives / Colin Greenstreet, unedited.\n"
        "Produced under the HTR API mini-project (ML-034), Recursive Archivist pilot v0.2, script recursive_archivist_v0.2_31102026.py.\n")
    with open(raw, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as f:
        f.write(hdr + "\n")
        for cid in order:
            d = rows[cid]; f.write(f"{VOLUME} {cid}\n")
            f.write(f"[page kind: {clean(d.get('page_kind'))}; date as written: {clean(d.get('date_as_written'))}; "
                    f"document type: {clean(d.get('document_type'))}; legibility: {clean(d.get('legibility'))}]\n")
            for ln in (d.get("transcription") or []):
                f.write(str(ln).rstrip() + "\n")
            f.write("\n")
    print(f"wrote {tsv.name} ({len(order)} rows) and {raw.name}")


USAGE = """usage: python recursive_archivist_v0.1_31102026.py <cmd> [args]
  download [all | n1 n2 ...]        read (hydrate) source images, log to logs/download_log.tsv
  prepare  [all | n1 n2 ...]        EXIF-rotate + resize to prepared-1568/
  submit   <tag> [all | n1 n2 ...]  create one batch; manifest in results/manifest_<tag>.json
  status   <tag>
  collect  <tag>                    save raw results, parsed.json, summary.json
  outputs  <tag> <version> <DDMMYYYY>   finding-aid TSV + RAW volume text"""


def parse_nums(args):
    if not args or args == ["all"]:
        return numbers()
    return [int(a) for a in args]


if __name__ == "__main__":
    a = sys.argv[1:]
    if not a:
        sys.exit(USAGE)
    c = a[0]
    if c == "download": cmd_download(parse_nums(a[1:]))
    elif c == "prepare": cmd_prepare(parse_nums(a[1:]))
    elif c == "submit": cmd_submit(parse_nums(a[2:]), a[1])
    elif c == "status": cmd_status(a[1])
    elif c == "collect": cmd_collect(a[1])
    elif c == "outputs": cmd_outputs(a[1], a[2], a[3])
    else: sys.exit(USAGE)

did generallye imploy seaven or eight men to
worke on board the said lighter for the weighing
of the said Ordnance and Iron and one
man constantly to bee and lye on board her
or soe neere on shoare that hee might bee —
within sight of the said lighter and looke to —
the same, and this respondent beleiveth the
said lighter did ride in such a place over the
said Racke [?] where shipps did and might ~~
usually passe unlesse they were of very great
burthen and the water lowe and then in
regard of the said Sunck vessell or racke [?]
they could not passe without very great
danger as hee beleiveth and he beleiveth that
at the tyme when the said shippe ye Mary ~~
came against the said lighter there was not
any person on board, but those employed by this
respondent were gone on shoare to a house very
neere the place where the said lighter did lye
where they had their dyet, \and/ a good distance
before the said shippe came neere to the said
lighter some of the \said/ persons employed very
same did see the said shippe and call to the
company thereof as he beleiveth wishinge —
them to take heed of the said lighter as hee
beleeveth, yet notwithstandinge the company
of the same out of carelesnesse or wilfulnesse
as he beleiveth did came [?] against the said —
lighter & breake her from her Moringes —
though there was roome for twenty shipps
as aforesaid to have gon on the one side —
& foure or five on the other all on breast
without doeinge or receivinge any damadge
as hee beleeveth and did carry her away at
least two or three miles as hee beleiveth by
wth this rondent [?] was very much damnified as
he beleiveth and otherwise he doth not beleive the
said posicon to bee true in any parte./

that the said Cable and Anchor are foule

amongst the goods in the same and that ~
respondent cannot possibly acquire or gett
the same againe as he beleiueth and ~
otherwise hee doth not beleiue the said prted

To the sixth he answereth and beleiueth that
the said vessell beinge soe unhappily driven
from her mooringes aforesaid was not

the dammadge aforesaid but was soone ~
after cast away which was cheifly occasioned
by reason of her beinge broken loose by the said
Cooque[?] and company in manner aforesaid
as he beleiueth and otherwise doth not beleiue
the same to bee true in any parte.
To the seaventh hee answereth and beleiueth
that if the said Cable could haue beene gotten
agayne the same might haue beene amended
& splized together agayne but it would haue
beene the worse by neere 10li[?] as he beleiueth
& otherwise referringe himselfe to his answers
to ye 1. 3. 5th [?] which hee beleiueth to bee true hee
doth not beleeue the same to bee true in any
parte.
To ye last he answereth that savinge his former
answers which he beleiueth to bee true hee doth

and was there stayed and forced to
~~Stay~~[?] serve the then Prince of Wales &

leeve the said pretended position to bee true
in any parte

that at the tyme when the said vessell was
seized in the Downes hee this rondent[?]

said fleete as hee beleeveth and was [illegible]

the said vessell came into the Downes
the time aforesaid when shee was seized
by the said revolted fleete without the
directions or knowledge of this respon
dent And otherwise hee doth not beleeve
the said pretended position to bee true in
any parte /

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8516
[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 3]

the Downes as aforesaid shee was forced
to bee and remaine in the revolted
Fleete untill such time as the Company
thereof could get an opportunity to
escape away with her againe which
was this respondents directions to
them that they should doe with the soo
nest as hee beleeveth And otherwise
hee for his pte doeth not beleeve the
said recited[?] position to bee true in any pte
To the 4th hee answereth and beleeveth
that the said shippe was kept and
detained in the said Fleete by the
space of five or six monethes as hee
beleeveth before shee could make an
escape being for a great pte of that
time locked upp within the Bridge
at Helvoe[?] sluce[?] that shee could not
possibly get away as hee beleeveth
hee beleeveth that dureing most pte of
that time the arlate Spark[?]s[?] was onbord
the said vessell and was taken out of her
by Command of the Prince or some
other of the Commanders in regard
hee endeavoured to make an escape with
her as hee beleeveth And otherwise
saving his former Answers hee doeth
not beleeve the said position to bee true
in any pte
To the 5th hee answereth That after the
said shipp had beene some few daies

~~pte~~ of the Prince of Wales his linnen

The personall answeres of Thomas
fflawes[?] made to the positions of an
allecon[?] given in against him on the

7. Article[?] doe followe /
To the first pretended position hee answereth
and referreth himselfe to the Charterpartie
for the voyadge in question exd in this cause
wch hee beleeueth to bee true, And otherwyse
for his parte hee doeth not beleeue the said
position to bee true in any part thereof /
To the second pretended position hee answereth
and beleeueth, that hee this rendent being at
the Canaries the voyadge in question was
there readye to receaue and take on board his
said shippe the unitye, all such goodes and
merchandizes as the said Mortons ffactors
would lade and putt on bord him, And was
there readye to haue staid out all the dayes
lymitted for his staye there by Charter ptie
but this rendent beleeueth that the arlate
Mortons ffactors, to whome this rendent was
consigned at the Canaries haueinge putt
on board this rendents shipp, ffiftye two
pipes of Canarye wynes and haueinge
noe more to lade for the said Mortons
Accompt as hee beleeueth did command
this rendent to bee gonne and sett sayle
therewth[?]

therewith for London and told him expressly
that hee had noe more to lade, unlesse hee

hee would protest against him, or to that
effect And thereupon this rendent beleeveth
hee did not staye at the Canaries, more
then fortye dayes vizt. from the seaventeenth
daye of October. 1650. unto the 26. of

hee doeth not beleeve the said posicon to bee
true in any part thereof

To the fifth pretended posicon hee answereth

The personall Answeres of Francis Aylmer [?]
made to the pretended positions of an allegacon
given against him on the behalfe of
Gilbert Leat Thomas Jennings & others
as followeth/
To the first hee answereth, that hee doth accept
the same soe farr forth as the same maketh for
him and not otherwise, and doth beleive that the
articlate mr Leate, and mr Jennings & Companye
did for the voyadge in controversye appoint
George Horsfall this respondents purser to bee
assisting to him in all things, according to
his duty, in keeping of Accompts signeing
of bills of Ladeing, disbursment of moneyes
for the said shipps use, & receiveing of fraight,
all which was to bee done, with the direction
and concurrence of him this respondent & not
otherwise as hee beleeveth, And otherwise
hee doth not beleeve the said pretended position
to bee true in any part/
To the second hee answereth and beleiveth
that the said George Horsfall being established
by the said mr Leate and Jennings & company
this respondents purser, to bee assisting unto
him in receiveing and payeing, as in the
answere to the precedent article is expressed,
the said George Horsfall, did for about
two yeares space receive all moneyes for
fraight, for goodes sould, or otherwise growing
due

said shipp & Company, and seuerall of the
mens wages, and the said Pursser being only
soe placed to bee assisting to him this rondent
as aforesaid, hee this rondent did desire him
the said Horsfall, to give him this rondent an
accompt, of the receipts and disbursments, for
the said voyadge, which hee the said Horsfall
did refuse to doe, as hee beleiueth, & thereupon
this rondent wished him, that if hee would not
give him a perticuler accompt, then hee should
deliuer him \[illegible]/ such moneyes, as hee had in
his hands, and thereupon the said George
Horsfall, did deliuer to him this rondent

beleeueth, & from and after that tyme this

in any part./

The psonall Answears [?] of John Harris and
John P[?]owit made to the pnted [?] positions [?],
of an Allegacon given and admitted against
them, on the behalfe of Edward Bellamy
and Thomas Day as followeth/
To the first they and each of them answereth, that
they nor either of them doth beleeve the saide
article to bee true in any part, Saveing that hee
the said John P[?]owit, doth beleeve that hee was
on board the said shipp at Gravesend before her
goeing out upon the said voyadge, to speake
with the men and take order about the saide
voyadge, and the said Edward Bellamy and
Thomas Day the ptes in this suite haveing
covenanted under theire hands and seales, to
make the said shipp sufficient and tight and
strong, and in and with all things necessarye
for the said voyadge, well tackled apparelled
and furnished, and Christopher Mitchell, one
of the undertakers with the said Bellamy,
and Day in Charter ptie, being to goe mr of the
said shipp for the said voyadge, and soe princi-
pally concerned to looke to the said shipps
sufficiencye and well furnishing wth all manner
of stores and necessaries, hee the said P[?]owit
did not conceive himselfe obliged, to take care
of the severall particulers (not then doubtinge
the exact pformance of the said Edward
Bellamy, Thomas Day and Xpo\te/fer [?] Mitchell
in the premisses, neither did hee take any-
(vide [?]

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8538
[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 4]
view or inspection of the said shipp, nor any
of her materialls or tackle, more then by his
coming on board as aforesaid, nor otherwise
accept or approve the same; then by letting
her proceede upon the said voyadge under the
conduct or comaunde of the said Mitchell
according to the Charter ptie, hee wth the
said Bellamy and Day having covenanted
as aforesaid that shee should bee tight and
sufficient, and in all things well furnished
and provided with tackle apparell and
necessaries as hee beleiveth./
To the second they and each of them answereth
that they and each of them beleiveth that
they did send out in the said shipp, when shee
proceeded on the said voyadge from Gravesend
one Carpenter and noe more, whoe did finde
the said shipp soe insufficient to performe
the said voyadge, as the said respondents have
heard and doe beleive insufficient, that hee
durst not adventure his life in the said
shipp, and did soe acquaint the said \after/[?] [illegible]
Mitchell (whoe went master of her) about
the tyme of her arrivall in the Downes, wth
his feare of her defects and his resolucion[?]
therefore not to proceede therein as they and
every of them beleiveth, and they beleive
the said Carpenter, did thereupon leave
the said shipp, att or about the Downes, but
did not take away any of the stores, of the
said

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8539
[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 4]
said shippe, or consentinge or imbarque the same

thereby lost her company, and there was a

in the said shippe to the Barbadoes and there

The personall answeares of John Harris[?]
and John Howitte[?], made to the pretended[?]

To the first they and either of them doth answere
and beleive that the said shippe the Little, -
George, was at the tyme of her goeing forth
upon the voyadge arlate, very rotten and -
insufficient to performe the said voyadge, and
soe much was taken notice of by divers -
that were upon her before her goeing forth
upon the same, and especially by the Carpen=
ters, and such as wrought upon her, as they &
every of them beleiveth, and they beleive that
some or one of those that did worke upon her, for
the prepareing her for the said voyadge, did informe
and acquaint the said Bellamy & Day, or one of
them with the said shippes defects, and that -
shee would not bee fitt & sufficient for the said
voyadge, or for men to adventure their lives in
unlesse shee was new sheathed & girdled, or to
that effect, yet notwithstanding the said
Bellamy and Day, did refuse or neglect to make
her sufficient, or to girdle or sheath her, as was
desired and necessary, as they and each of them
beleiveth, and did only besmeare over[?] the said
shippe with ground stuffe & brimstone to make
her seeme faire to the eye, but the same was
soe rotten and leakye and insufficient that
divers ~~and~~[?] some of the Company left her

The Personall answeres of Thomas Jenni[illegible]
and Gilbert Keate[?] made to the posicions
of an Allegation given in against
them on the behalfe of ffrancis
Ashmore doe followe.
To the first pretended posicion they
answere and beleeve that within the time
arlate these respondents were owners of
of five, sixteenes & a twelfth part of
the said Shipp the Blessinge arlate
and of her tackle apparell & furniture
& noe more[?] and soe [illegible] accompted[?],
And the said Ashmore was alsoe
owner and proprietor of one sixteene
part of the said Shipp, and the said
Ashmore was within the said time as
hee beleeveth appointed Master of the
said Shipp by these respondents and the
rest of her owners And otherwise for
his part they doe not beleeve the said
posicion to be true in any part thereof
To the second pretended posicion they
answere & beleeve that about the
moneth of May 1647. and not before
as they beleeve, the said Shipp the
blessinge by the order of these respondents
and other her owners was sett out to
Sea on a tradinge voyage under the
Comand of the said Ashmore to ~
severall portes and places beyond the
Seas and soe for London againe And
the said Shipp did begin the said ~
imployment, and the Master and
Companie of her entred into pay on
or about the 20th of May 1647.
and not before as they beleeve, And
the said ffrancis Ashmore and the
other parties before menconed went
in her & served in her as they beleeve
But whether the said parties were the
said Ashmores servants or noe these
respondents knowe not, or howe longe
they served or for what wages, nor
knowe what to beleeve in regard the
said businesse concerninge the said
voyage was solely managed by
Captaine Pranley[?], and these respondents
knowe noe particulers thereof And

[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 4]
otherwise for their ptes they nor either
of them doe not beleeve the said posicion
to be true in any pte thereof
To the third pretended posicion and
Schedule therein menconed they and
every of them answere & beleeve that
they doe referr themselves to their
former answeres, and otherwise for
their ptes they doe not beleeve the
said posicion to be true in any pte
thereof.
To the fourth pretended posicion
they answere and accept the contents
of the said posicion soe farr forth
as the same doth make for them & not
otherwise And they beleeve that
these respondents [?] and the rest of the
owners of the said Shipp did put
in and imploye the arlate Horsfall
to goe Purser in the said Shipp &
voyage wth Commission to receive
and pay all thinges dureinge the sd
voyage, and that the said Ashmore
was not to meddle with either re[?]
receavinge or payinge But these
respondents [?] beleeve that in the said
voyage the said Ashmore without
any order contrary to these
respondents [?] & Companies directions
did turne out the said Horsfall
and tooke away from him to the

wch were the proper moneys of
these respondents [?] and Companies
and converted the same to his
owne use and never gave these
respondents [?] any Accompt of the same
as they beleeve And otherwise

The personall answeres of George Chambers [?]
made to the allegation given into this Court
on the parte and behalfe of Samuell Sh[illegible]
and others doe follow.
To the said allegation hee answereth and beleeveth
that hee this respondent being owner of the shipp
the George, did lett the said shipp to freight to
certayne merchants of London, for a voyadge from
London to Porto Porto in Portugall, and soe to
London agayne, at 3li 5s 6d [?] p tonne, for every tonne
of goods the said shipp should bringe home, from
Porto Porto to London, and not otherwise as hee
beleeveth, the fraight thereof to bee paid att the
said shipps arrivall and discharge here and, not
before. And this respondent beleeveth that, upon the
said shipps letting to freight, soe as aforesaid
hee this respondent or the master of the said shipp
by his order did hire the said partyes, at the
monethly rates expressed in the said Allegation
to serve in the said shipp, from this Port of
London to Porto Porto and soe backe agayne
to London, and not otherwise, and at their safe
bringinge the said shipp and ladeing to this
Porte of London and not before, the said partyes
were to receave their wages as hee beleeveth
this respondent beleeveth, that the said ptie [?] did goe
out in the said shipp, and entred into pay the
eight of May allegate, and safely arrived att
Porto Porto, and there delivered the said shipps
outward Ladeinge, within the space [?] of nyneteene [?]
dayes

dayes after the said shipps departure from -
London, and afterwards this rondent beleeveth,
the said master and Company tooke in theire
homeward Ladeinge of oyle and other goods
for the Accompt of the fraighter and others,
and came therewith contrary to order, without
direction or advise, into the Porte of Plymouth -
beinge noe porte where the same was to bee
discharged, about the 18. or 19. of August
last [?] past [?], and there staid, when they might have
come to [?] for London, of wch this rondent havinge
notice, and beinge bound by Charter partie not
to neglect any opportunitye of wynde & wether
did write vnto them, if it were possible to come
a Convoye to come awaye therewith for London,
and if there were noe Convoye, then vnlesse hee
had certayne notice that the Coast were cleere
not to come away from thence And not other-

that about the 5th day of August last, the
said marriners did bringe the said shipp and

in their passage thither this rondent beleeveth
the said Company before they came to Dover-
were tould by one whoe kept the lights that

Georg[?] Chambers

The personall Answers of John Lym[?]
Mr of the shipp Sampson to a certaine
Schedule of wages on the behalfe of
Lawrence Martinson[?] Ede[?] Peterson and
others as followeth.
To the allegacon in the Acts and Schedule
therein mentioned he answereth and beleeveth
that the severall persons in the said Schedule
mentioned did serve in the said Shipp for
the tyme in the said Schedule hereunto annexed
expressed, and were to have the severall
respective monethly wages in the said
Schedule annexed conteyned, and have
had and received in part thereof the
severall and respective sumes in the
said Schedule sett downe and in the
said voyadge from Lisborne to this
port of London severall Merchants
whoe laded goods doe demaund damages
from this respondent and Companye for
goods Embezelled and damnified to the
value of fower hundred poundes or
thereabouts wch damadge the saide
persons Schedulate are equally lyable
unto the rest of the Marryners as
he beleeveth wch if they can cleare
themselves from he is ready to pay
them the residue of their said wages
and otherwise he doth not beleeve the
said Act or Schedule therein mentioned
to be true in any parte.   Jo[?]hn Lin[?]d[?]

The p[er]sonall answeares of Thomas Millard
and John Ysterre[?], marriners of the shipp
the Sampson made to a pretended &
allegacon given against them and ye[?]
company on the behalfe of Captayne
John Lindes as followeth vizt.
To the first and second positions of the said pretended
allegacon they answere and beleeve that in the
moneth of August 1650. and not before as they
beleeve, and on or about the first or seaventh day of
the same moneth, the shippe the Sampson arlate
was seized by the officers of the Kinge of Portugall
vpon the Commaund of the said Kinge as they
beleeve, and the said Captayne John Lindes and
some of the said shipps Company, were putt and
kept out of the said shipp, for the space of
seaventeene monethes and fourteene dayes and
noe longer as they beleeve vizt vntill the twen-
tieth day of January 1651. arlate, but these
respondents, Thomas Millard and John Ysteere,
and alsoe Robert Sheffeild, Roger Roberts, Edmund
Cripe[?], Richard Powell[?], Samuell Ward and John
Andrewes parties in this suite were not at all
putt out of the said shipp, but remayned, and
continued in her all the said tyme, and by the
appointment and direction of the said Captayne
John Lyndes, did performe theire duty and service
therein, And they further beleeve, that it hath
beene and is a constant vse and custome at the
Baihia where the \said/ shippe the Sampson was
seized as aforesaid, for all English shippes and
other forraigne shippes presently vpon theire
arrivall there to carry theire sayles and yards
on shoare, and that according to the saide
Custome

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8562
[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 4]
Custome, the sailes of the said shippe the
Sampson, were shortly after her arrivall there
carryed on shoare, but the said Captayne Lynt [?]
refusinge to carry his yards on shoare, hee onely
and none other of his company, was for his
contempt therein imprisoned for the space of
two daies at the most and not above, and then
the yards of the said shipp beinge carryed on
shoare the said Captayne Lynd was presently
released, and continewed in the Command &
possession of the said shipp, untill the said
sixt or seaventh day of August 1650. And they
further beleeve, that at the tyme when the said
shippe was seized as aforesaid, by the officers
of the Kinge of Portingall, shee was fully
freighted with goods to bee delivered at Lisbon,
some of them consigned to English Marchants
and the rest to Portingueses, and that none of
the said goods, were taken out of the said shippe
untill shee arrived at Lisbon, whither the said
shippe was bound and carryed And upon her
arrivall there, all her ladinge was delivered
out of her to the severall persons to whome they
were consigned, aswell to the English as to the
Portugueses and the freight due for the same
goods was fully paid, and was wholly imployed
and laid out in discharge and satisfaction of
moneys laid out upon the said shipp, and for
her use, shee beinge very leaky and much Ex [?]
decayed and defective, when shee was soe
seized and besides the said freight, the said
Captayne Linde had and received as they
beleeve of and from the Kinge of Portingall or
some

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8563
[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 3]
or some of his officers by his appoyntment &
for and in satisfaction of the losse of the use &
imployment of the said shipp from the tyme
\fifteene thousand Crownes or some other considerable some of/
of her said seizure the summe of money, and
soe much the said Captayne Lines [?] hath severall
tymes, or at least once as they beleeve, con-
fessed and acknowledged, And they alsoe beleeve
that after the release of the said shipp, from
the said seizure vizt after the expiracon of the
said seaventeene monethes and fowreteene dayes
the Kinge of Portugall, or his officers did pay
off the Portugaile marriners imployed in the
said shipp the seaventeene monethes and fowre-
teene dayes, and did offer to pay, and would
have paid these respondents, and the other English
marriners in the said shipp the [illegible] for their
wages due for theire service in the said shipp
the said seaventeene monethes and fowreteene
dayes, but the said Captayne Lines [?] did oppose
and withstand the payment of the said wages,
and affirmed that they were not to have any
wages, untill the returne of the said shipp into
England, and thereupon these respondents, and the
said other English marriners did not receive
and were not paid their said wages, which
otherwise had bin paid them as they beleeve
but the said Captayne Lines [?] had and received
their said wages, for the said tyme or satisfaction
for the same as they beleeve, And otherwise
they doe not beleeve the said posicons or either
of them to bee true in any part of them.
To the third posicon of the said pretended acon
they answere, and doe not beleeve the same to
bee

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8564
[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 3]
bee true in any parte thereof savinge theire
former answeare./
To the last position they answeare and beleeve
what they have herein formerly beleeved, and
deny what they have formerly denyed./
Thomas Mill[illegible]

The personall answeares of Captayne
John Lynes, made to the pretended
positions of an allegation given against
him, on the behalfe of Millard,
Sheffeild and others as followeth./
To the first hee answereth and beleeveth, that
the said shipp was seized by the Officers
of the Kinge of Portugall at the Bahia in the
Brazeel upon or about the twentith of June
1649, and hee was kept out of the said shipp
and the possession detayned from him, from
that tyme till the 20 of Janu[?] 165[?]
as hee beleeveth, and otherwise hee doth not
beleeve the said pretended position to bee true in
any part./
To the second hee answereth that hee refe=
rreth ~~[illegible]~~ the contents of the same, soe farre as
they make for him, and beleeveth that the
Portugalls after the seizure of the said
shipp did continue the said pt[?]ed parlat[?]
on board the said shipp to helpe and assist

The personall answere of Thomas Per[?]ke[?] &[?]
John Maddocke made to the positions
of a reformed[?] Libell given against
them and company on the behalfe of
william Thomas as followeth viz.
To the first second and third positions
of the said reformed[?] Libell they answer
and either of them doth answer and beleeve
that in or about the monethes of Aprill or
May in the yeare of or Lord 1642 the respondte[?]
and company being lawfull owners and
proprietors of the ~~said~~ Shi[?] the Charles
arlate, and in the quiet and peaceable
possession thereof did freight and set out the
said Shipp from the port of Bristoll being then
in the possession of the Parliamt on a voyage
to some of the Carrebby[?] Ilands and to returne
againe to the port of Bristoll, and that after
the departure of the said Shipp on[?] the said
voyage and before her returne againe into
England the Citty and port of Bristoll
were taken and surprized by the late Kinge
and his party and continued in theire
possession untill the returne of the said
Shipp from the said voyage, and for

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8596
[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 3]
some time after, And they also beleive that in
or about the monethes of October or Novemb[?]
1643[?] the said Shipp the Charles returned from
her foresaid voyage, and the master and company
of the said Shipp verily beleeving that Bristoll
had bin then in the Parliamts possession as
it was when they sett saile from Genua[?] with
the said Shipp on the outward voyage, and not
knowinge or haveinge heard any thinge of the
takinge thereof by the late King and his
pty did carry the said Shipp with her tackle
furniture and ladinge into Kingroade in
the Port of Bristoll, and there the said
Shipp with her tack[?] furniture & ladinge
was within a short time after her arrivall
there as these respondts beleive taken and
seized by some of the late Kings ptye
as prize and was violently kept and deteyned
from these respondts & company untill the
moneth of October 1645 or thereabouts
att wch time the said Citty of Bristoll
was besieged, and within a few dayes after
was retaken by the Parliamts forces from
the late Kinge and his ptye and they
also beleive that att the tyme of the

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8597
[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 3]
seizure of the said Shipp and her tackle
furniture and lading by the late Kings pty
as aforesaid and for all the greatest pte of the
tyme that Bristoll was in the possession of
the late Kinge and his pty the arlate [?] William
Thomas was in Bristoll and had some office
or place of Comand either in the fleet or
the army of the said Kinge, and that the said
Shipp with her tackle and furniture after shee
was soe seized by the late Kings pty did

beleeve, within a few dayes ~~and~~ before the

forces from the late Kinge and his pty as
aforesaid. But by what meanes or uppon

pte [?] of her or of her tackle & furniture
and what money hee soe paid if any these
respondts [?] for theire owne partes and the

altogether ignorant for that they these
respondts [?] and all the rest of theire company
for all the tyme that Bristoll remained in

pty

Seas, they the said Powell, Reynolds and

arlate one whereof was plundered
(as he beleeveth) by ye arlate George
Blowers and some others of which this
respondent was none, and brought on
board the shippe arlate by them, the other
bedd as he beleeveth was brought on
board ye shippe arlate by one Thomas
Palmer[?] and those two bedds were brought
on board ye shippe arlate at Dundee in
Scotland to bee carried from thence to
London (as he beleeveth) and as to ye rest of
ye

ye article he answereth and beleeveth ye
same to bee true.

by lawe to make any answeare to ye pretended
position in regard ye ~~[illegible]~~ charge therein (as he
humbly conceiveth) is criminall, yet for
satisfaction of this Court he answereth that
he doth not beleeve ye same to bee true in any
parte./

goods arlate wherein hee was ordered to
pay as, in ye acts orders & decrees of this Court

To ye last he answereth and beleeveth ~~as~~
what is by him beleeved and denyeth what is

To the first p'sed position he answeareth that he
accepteth the same soe farre forth as the same maketh
for him, and not otherwise; and beleeveth that within
the tyme arlate, the arlate Watson, James Salmon[?]
and Humfrey[?] Bane[?] named in the Charter partie
bearinge date the nynth day of Aprill 1652 have
for the voyage in question havinge (as he beleeveth)
occasion for a Shipp to goe to Greenland upon the
ffishinge designe for oyle, they or some of them
did come to this rendent beinge master, and part
owner of the said Shipp the Dove arlate, and
desired this rendent that he would lett the said
Shipp to fraight unto them for the said voyage
and this rendent beleeveth that in conference
had with them or some of them thereabout
he this rendent did say (as he beleeveth the
truth was) that the said Shipp was a fittinge
Shipp for the voyage, and a stronge and
\that he did desire or intreate the said Watson & company[?]/
\or any of them to imploy the said Shipp & otherwise doth X/
[margin] X not beleeve
sufficient Shipp but he doth not beleeve ^ the
p'sed position to be true in any part.
To the second, third and fowerth positions of the
said p'sed allegacon he answeareth and saith
that the Shipp arlate some fewe weekes before
her goeinge to sea the voyage in question did
come out of the docke, where she was well and
sufficiently repayred and fitted to goe to sea
and that in such her repayringe, and fittinge
of her this rendent, and the rest of the owners
did lay out upon the said Shipp the some of
five hundred and ffiftie pounds sterlinge, and
the

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8620
[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 4]
the said shipp did not after such her repayringe
and fittinge goe out of the river of Thames, before
she went the voyage in question And he beleeveth
that the said shipp was the tyme arlate a stronge
and staunch shipp and usefull and fitt for the
voyage, and imployment arlate, and as to the
covenante and agreemente arlate he referreth –
himselfe to the schedule hereunto annexed which
(as he beleeveth) the true coppie of the originall
charter partie made for the voyage arlate, the
Counter parte whereof is in the hande and possession
of the said Watson as he beleeveth, and he ~

and that the said charter partie was and is signed

was and is to pay unto him this Rendent[?] seaventy
pounde p month for the said shipp for every

The personall Answers of Joseph
Dobbins made to the pretended positions
of an allegation given against him in the
names of Isaack Gould Henry Roach
and company as followeth./
To the first second third and the rest of ye
articles of the said allegation he answereth
that since the voyadge mencioned in the said
allegation and severall articles thereof this
respondent hath given to the said Gould &
Roach who were then part owners of the
said shippe a iust and true accompte of
all the profitts of the said voyadge which
did belonge to them as hee beleeveth and
hath payd them whatsoever was due for
ye same as hee beleeveth, and they the said
Gould & Roach have subscribed this said
accompts and put their names to this
rondents booke and fully acquitted and
discharged him this rondent from any
further pretence or demaund for or
concerninge ye said voyadge as hee
beleeveth and the said Gould and Roach
have alsoe since sold all their right title
& interest to the said shippe the Hester [?]
to him this rondent and therefore savinge
his former answeares made to the positions
of a libell formerly given against him on
ye behalfe of the said Gould & Roach wch hee
beleeveth to bee true hee doth humbly
conceive hee is not bound by lawe otherwise to
make answer to ye said positions or any of
them.
Joseph Dobins

The personall answere of J[?] [?]larke [?] ~
Stephens [?] his answere [?] made to the positions of a
Libell given in against him on the ~
behalfe of George Searle the elder
and John Searle doe followe /
To the first pretended position hee answereth
and accepteth the contents of the said position
soe farr as the same doe make for him and
not otherwise and beleeveth that this tyme
articulate this respondent and the articulate Alford
did obtayne letters of reprizall agt the
ffrench to the value of 5000li or there abouts
as hee beleeveth, from the Councell of State /
To the second pretended position hee answereth
and beleeveth the same to bee true /
To the third pretended position hee answereth,
and referreth himselfe to the actes articulate
remayninge in the registry of this Courte
wch hee beleeveth to bee true, and further

To the first and second positions the said Henry
ffisher doth answeare and beleeue that hee the
said ffisher, did goe to the howse of the said
Greene, to speake with him about his being
hired for the voyage in controversie and some
after his coming to him, and some small
conference there about, the said Greene came
out of his house and walked forth with him, And

Greene did agree with him to goe in the said
shippe the said voyage in controversie, from
this

[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 4]

without makeing any perticuler agreement
~~[illegible]~~, or soe much at [illegible] [?]
to his the said Fishers nowe remembrance
att any tyme or place where or when hee should

who also went in the said vessell the saide
voyadge, being desirous, that hee the said Ball,
should speake with the said Greene, brought him
word that hee was in Little Eastcheape, and
thereupon hee the said John Ball, went unto

Cheape eating of beefe and did there make
agreement with him for his said servant, John
Phelpes to goe with him the said voyadge, from
this port of London to newe England, from
thence to the Barbadoes, and from thence to
this port of London, for wch service hee the

Ball, the some of thirty shillings p moneth,
absolutely without any provisoe or other condicion
And they doe severally and respectively answeare

goeing for lesse, for severall yeares before

To the third they answeare that they nor
either of them doe beleeve the same doth
conteyne any thinge of truthe therein but is
a meere pretence of the said Greene to hinder
the mariners from theire iust wages, and to
putt them to vniust [?] vexacon, to enforce them
to a composition, and by that and indirect wayes
hath soe wearyed out divers of them, that they
have taken about foure monthes pay for
thirteene — monthes service, and soe much
hee the said Greene hath proffered to them
the said ffisher and Ball, but the rest being
as they conceive soe rightly due to them and
soe unduly deteyned, they doe refuse to accept
the same./
To the fourth, they and each of them answeareth
& beleeveth, that the said shipp did goe from
this Port of London to New England & there
safely arrived and delivered her goods, & from
thence to the Barbadoes, and there alsoe
safely arrived and delivered her goodes, and
there was freighted for England, and in her
course thither was mett with and taken
by three or more Dutchmen of warre, & the
shippe & lost to the owners thereof, And
otherwise they nor either of them doth beleeve
the said arte [?] to bee true in any parte./

[margin] Repeated before
made to the positions or Articles of a Libell
[margin] Dr. Clerke and
given agt. him on the behalfe of Robert
[margin] Cha: [illegible] [illegible]
Girlinge as followeth./
[margin] Esq.
To the first hee answeareth, That he beleiveth
the same to bee true./
To the second and third hee answeareth, and
beleiveth, That in or about the tyme, in the said
Article expressed, James Salmon in the said
Charter ptye ~~[illegible]~~ mentioned, did hire the
said shippe of the said Robert Girlinge, for the
voyadge in the said Charter ptie declared,
And then hee the said Salmon, did lett & sett[?] over[?]
Sixteene partes of the said shippe, to him the
said Richard Watson, and twoe sixteene partes
to one Grey[?] Deane[?], alsoe in the said Charter
ptye specified, for and after the rates therein
conteyned; and after hee the said Richard Watson
had putt his goods and provisions on board, for
the said voyadge, extendinge to neere the value
of Seaven hundred pounds, the said Girlinge
brought the said Charter ptye to the said
Richard Watsons house, when the said shippe
was

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8741
[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 4]
was readye to departe vpon the said voyadge, -
and told him vnlesse \[illegible]/ hee would seale the saide
Charter ptie, the said vessell should not proceede
or budge, whereupon the said mr Watson being -
more sitated [?] and streightned, the tyme of the -
yeare being farre spent, and his goods being on
board (notwithstanding hee had diverse \tymes/ told him
the said Girling, before, that hee would not seale
the said Charter ptie, especiallie for more then
for seaven sixteenth parts, wch hee had hired,,
from the said Salmon) yett being then exacted [?] -
vpon by the said Girling) hee did signe and seale -
the said Charter ptie, vpon or about the fifth of
May, and not before, and the word Richard \Watson/ att the
bottome of the said Charter ptie are written, wth
his proper hand, and being constrayned as afore-
said, by his goods being on board, hee did deliver -
the said Charter ptie, for his Act and deede,
And otherwise hee doeth not beleive the said ~~[illegible]~~
Articles, or either of them to bee true in any
parte. /
To the fourth hee answeareth and beleiveth, That
hee the said Richard Watson, having soe sealed
the said Charter ptie, vpon the fifth of May
aforesaid, the said shipp did the same day, or the
next daye after, sett sayle towards Greenland
and did in the passage thither prove soe leakye
and insufficient as hee beleiveth, That the company
were inforced, to take extraordinary paynes to
keepe her above water, but did att length wth
great toyle and labour, in company gett her
(to

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8742
[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 3]
to Greenland, where \some of/ the Company were also
enforced ~~[illegible]~~ \a place/ to lay her, upp to stopp her leakes ~~[illegible]~~
~~leakes~~ and amend her, being resolved there to have
Lefte her, by reason of her insufficiencye, as hee
beleiveth; soe that hee doeth beleive for his part
hee had noe service att all of the said shipp att
Greenland, but rather much difference[?] and
hinderance; for the company were soe spent and
~~[illegible]~~ \wearied/ with continuall pumpinge and bringinge
her thither, and soe hindered and obstructed with
looking after her, and amending her, when shee
was there, that they had little or noe tyme to
looke after the Catchinge of whales which was
the designe upon which the said shipp and
companye were sent, yett hee doeth beleive,
that by the helpe of her amendment, and the
great and extraordinarye care, and pumpeing
of the companye, the said shippe did returne
home agayne, about the moneth of September
in the said Article deduced, but during all
the tyme the said shippe was out, the said
Richard Watson, had noe other service of her
but onlye his victualls and wages consumed
for carryinge out and returning the said shippe
the dove[?] being soe leakye, ~~[illegible]~~ and
insufficient as aforesaid, but for the exact
tyme of her arrivall att Greenland, and settinge
sayle from thence, hee being not there, or any
wayes privie thereunto hee cannot answeare
And otherwise saving that hee referreth him
selfe to the Contents of the said Charter
ptie, hee doth not beleive the said Article
to bee true in any part /

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8743
[page kind: text page; date as written: 2. Sept. 165[?]; document type: personal answer; legibility: 4]
To the fifth and sixth and the rest of the Articles
the said Richard Batson doth answere, that hee
verilye beleiveth, that by the rottenesse and, &
defects of the said shippe this voyadge, \and/
designe was overthrowne, and hee and companye
were thereby dammified at least seaven or eight
hundred pounds as hee beleiveth, for wch hee, &
beleiveth the said Girling ought not onlye to bee
debarred of any freight or hire for the said shippe
but alsoe bee compelled to make satisfaccion for
the said damadges, yet notwithstanding the
said Girlinge hath impleaded and sued the said
Richard Batson, for the said pretended freight
in this Court, which hee doth refuse, for iust
cause as hee beleiveth to pay, and otherwise
saving that hee is a subiect of this Common=
wealth, and lyable to the Lawes thereof, hee
doth not beleive the said positions or either of
them to bee true in any part.
Richard Batson

15o Septemb 1653
The personall Answeares of William Pyle[?]
made to \the/ Allegation in the Acts of Court
on the behalfe of Stemill[?]          Turner
and others as Administratrs of the goods
of Mathew Stemill[?] and others &c, as
followeth/
To the said Allegation hee answeareth that hee
hee referreth himselfe to his answeres formerlye
made in this Court to the demandes made
therein on the behalfe of the said Stemyll and
others, wch hee beleeveth to bee true, and for further
satisfaction of this Court, in the demands of
the said parties, though hee was not master
of the said Vessell, yet hee doth beleive the
said Stemill, Sanders & Tindall[?], did goe forth
and serve in the said voyage, & were to have had
the monethly wages expressed in the said
schedule, and did enter into paye about the tyme
specified there, and the said William Pyle[?]
doth beleive that hee being a parte Owner of
the said shippe the mr thereof, Henry Pulman[?]
did bringe some of the Company to him, to
acquaint him with the hire of them, and that
they might knowe from him, the condicion of the
voyage, and thereupon hee did acquainte them
with the termes of the said voyage, and did
give to each of them, that was soe brought to
him, a noate under his hand, as in his said
former answeares are expressed, but hee did not
write downe the names of such to whome hee did
give

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8753
[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 4]
give such noates, and therefore hee cannot
directly declare to whome uppon his oath, but
beleeveth the ptie that [illegible] hee, have such
his noates soe given, to wch hee shall willinglye
submit, but hee doth beleive the said Stennill[?] &
Sanders were twoe of the ptie that had such
noates, and hee doth beleive they were to have
gone to Guiney to fetch Negroes, and to have
brought the same to the Canaries, but before
they came from Guiney, or brought any Negroes
or other goods from thence to any place, they
runne the said shippe the Swanne on shoare, in or \neare/
the said partes of Guiney, and cast the same
away, and lost both stock and block, the same
being of the burthen of three hundred tonnes and
upwards, and having twentye Gunnes in her and
fourteene monethes victualls, when shee went
out, all wch were worth, twoe thousand foure
hundred poundes as hee beleeveth, the wch hee and
the rest of the owners have lost, and not received
one pennye freight, soe that hee doth not conceive
there is any wages due to the said parties, and
hee doth beleive the said Stennill[?] Sanders and
Tindall[?], dyed in the said voyage at Guiney,
but when they dyed, or to what place they went
in Guiney, the said Pyle being not in the said
voyadge, cannott further answeare.
Wm Pyle

was the sayd 30th of October seized & taken
by Prince Rupert and by him Carried away

the sayd shippe together with her lading

it And he further believeth that hee this

as were worth 300li and not more as he believeth

To the ffifth[?] p'tended Article hee answereth &
beleeveth, that hee this respondent hath not payd
nor satisfied the sayd Lowell[?] and Company
their p'tended wages neither doth hee beleeve
that hee ought by law to doe the same, but
hee beleeveth that hee was at their suite
arrested by a warrant out of this Court,
and that hee hath putt in good bayle to
answere their p'tended action And otherwise
hee doth not beleeve the sayd article to bee true
in any part thereof./
To the last hee answereth & beleeveth his
former answere to bee true, and otherwise doth
not beleeve the sayd Article to bee true in
any p'te./

The p'sonall answeares of William

To the first p'tended arte they and either of them
answeare & beleeve that the sayd shipp the
Recovery, did discharge her outward bound
Lading, at the Barbadoes, where shee receivd
her homeward bound Lading, wch shee began
to take in upon the 26th of May .1652. and
(not

her homeward Ladeinge, to witt on the 13th day

to dischardge the same and otherwise shee
doth not beleive the said arte to bee true in

To the second arte of the said Allegacon they &

to bee true./
To the third ptended[?] arte, they and either of them

aboard, when in truth hee himselfe went on

said was a Pilott, but in reall truth, hee was
a very unskillfull pson[?], and not wayes[?]

To the 9th added arte hee answeareth & beleiveth
that duringe such tyme, as the saide shipp
the Recoverye, and some parte of her Ladinge
but not all, did remayne att Antegoe, and that
the same was puttinge out, into other Vessells
to bee carryed for London, Prince Rupert came
thither with his shippes, and seized upon
all the Ladeinge that was left, and tooke
away the whole Loadeinge, from Captayne
Powell, & carryed the same away with him, &
robbed and dispoyled the said Swift and his
imployers of the same, and the said shipp
was there left, & could not have bine gott
from thence, soe as both shipp & what goodes
was in her, were utterly lost to the owners
of the same, but they & either of them well
knowe that the said shipp the Recovery
might have laden all the goodes, that was
in her aboard the shippe the Exp[?]well, wch
shipp arrived at Antego upon the 13th day
of October 1652. and came thither for that
purpose

called) for the space [?] of about these

and to Willyn [?] overde [?] Rinald [?] Erix [?] and
this deponent dwellinge, and to noe
other person or place wthin the
dominions of the States of the vnited
Provinces or subiects of the ffrench [?]
Kinge and that the said shipp did
really and truely begunne this her
voyage from the Citty of Ham=
brough scituate vpon or neare
the River of Elbe, and did there
take into her the goods nowe
seized in her and at noe other
place wthin the Jurisdiction
of the States of the vnited
Provinces, and when he the
said [illegible] did make mention
of Hambrough, hee did and doth
thereby meane, and truely and
plainely intend the Cittye of
Hambrough vnder the

States of Hambrough, and
had not nor hath any equivocall

not beleive the said articles to
be true in any part savinge

dutch manner of buildinge.—

The personall answers of Jo[illegible]

libell hee answereth and beleeveth that upon

the sayd Abraham Smith did according to the
agreement afore demand the \two/ Negroes [?]
of this deponent or the value thereof in goods
And that thereupon this deponent did give order
to the afore William Cooke to pay unto the
sayd Abraham Smith ~~[illegible]~~ such a quantity
of Sugars as the sayd two Negroes were worth
and should be sold for But denyeth that hee
this deponent gave any order to the sayd Cooke
to pay the sayd Smith three thousand weight

two Negroes the quantity of two thousand
eight hundred and twenty weight of Sugars
aforesd[?] but denying that it was by this

Brooke had given up his Accompts unto
this rendent by which it did appeare And
\otherwise[?]/ this rendent doth not beleeve the sayd
position to be true in any part thereof
To the seaventh position of the sayd pretended
libell hee answereth and beleeveth that ye

were on board the said shipp did
come with the said Charter party
in Company of the Scrivener who[?]
made the same - - vnto the said Bathons
howse lodge[?] (as he beleeveth) the
place appointed as aforesaid
for the sealinge thereof And
otherwise he doth not beleeve
this pretended position to be true
in any part
To the last he answereth and
confesseth what is by him
confessed, and denieth what
is by him denyed /
Robert C[illegible]
