[margin] before all the
[margin] Judges
The personall Answeares of Captayne
Henry H[?]well, made to the pretended [?] positions [?]
of a Libell given against him, on the
behalfe of Robert Llewellyn [?] Peter
Mathewe [?], James Janey [?], & Valentyne
Austen as followeth –
To the first and second hee answeareth That
hee accepteth and beleiveth the same to bee true./
To the third hee answeareth and beleiveth, That
on or about the tyme specified, in the said article
hee did sayle, in the said Shippe as mr, from the
Barbadoes to Antegoe, there to receive & take
the ladding of a Shippe, called the Recoverie
whereof William Swift was Commander (wch
was unhappily wracked upon the Rocks at Antegoe)
into the said vessell the Hopewell, and then was
from thence to goe to Nevis, and soe to returne to
this Port of London, and hee beleiveth hee was to
make what convenyent speed hee could, as all
masters are at all Ports and places, and not to
staye and spend their tyme unnecessarily, and hee
beleiveth hee went for noe other cause to Antegoe
aforesaid, but to take in the goods out of the Recoverie
into the Hopewell, And otherwise hee doth not
beleive the said Article to bee true, in any parte./
To the fourth, fifth, sixth and seaventh hee answeareth
and beleiveth, That hee with the said Shippe did
arrive at Antegoe, upon the fourteenth day of
October. 1652. in the afternoone and not before
and the said vessell came from the Barbadoes
aforesaid

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8804
[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 4]
aforesaid where shee had bine much dryed with
the Sunne, and prejudicated by the wormes, hee
was necessitated to spend two dayes in the
careeninge [?] of the said vessell, and takeing up
severall peices of her Lower waile [?] & puttinge
in new peeces, and the seaventeenth beinge the
Lords day, they did forbeare any worke, & upon the
eighteenth the said vessell the Hopewell was hayled
on board, or to to the side of the Recovery, and that
day hee the said Powell and company, did take
out some of the Lower teare of the butts which
were brought in her from the Barbathoes, and
heaved out some of the Ballast, and tooke out some
of the Gunns of the Recovery into the Hopewell
and on the nineteenth tooke in the rest of the
Gunns and stowed them in the said vessell the
Hopewell, And the said Swift the Commander
of the said vessell the Recovery would not deliver
him, or lett him take in any of the goods, out of the
Recovery into the Hopewell, untill such tyme as
hee had first taken in the said Gunns, soe that
it was the twentith day of the said moneth of
October. 1652. before the said vessell could beginne
to take in the said Sugar, or other goods out of the
Recovery, and hee the said Powell and company,
did for all the tyme of his beinge there doe his
utmost indeavour to gett the said goods on board,
and made all convenient speede they could there=
abouts, and they did not neglect any tyme as hee
beleiveth from the doeing thereof, and hee beleiveth
that when the said shipps was careeninge hee
sent some of the men to cutt wood to stowe caske [?]
with

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8805
[page kind: text page; date as written: ; document type: personal answer; legibility: 4]
which was necessarie to bee done, for they could not
have stowed the Caske well without wood as hee
beleiveth and hee beleiveth that once when hee had
sett the men to worke, hee did goe with two or three
of the boyes to haule [?] the nett for turtles, but did
not stay one hower thereabouts as hee beleiveth
[margin] [dotted mark]
and hee doeth beleive that only some part of the
lading, that was onboard the Recovery, and not
all, was taken out of the said Vessell the Recovery
into the Hopewell att severall tymes, but the same
was not for want of the pformance of any duty
of him the said Howell [?], or any of his company,
but because they could not wthin that tyme effect
the same as hee beleiveth And otherwise hee doth
not beleive the said Articles, or either of them to
bee true in any parte
To the 8th hee answeareth and beleiveth that,
whilest hee was with the said Vessell at Antegoe
aforesaid takeing in the goods, out of the foresaide
Shippe the Recovery, there was a Spanish Vessell
cast away upon the Rocks neere Barbadoes [?], about
eight leagues distance, and there was some sent
came downe from thence to desire helpe to fetch
of the Passingers and company from the Rocks,
and save some of their goods, promiseing good
satisfaction, to such as would assist them, and
they did declare that they were good quantities
of Pitch and many very rich goods and there=
upon the said Vessell. the Hopewell having
great occasion for pitch to caulke her to make
her to make her fitt for her homeward voyadge,
and none being to bee had att Antegoe, hee
(the

the said Powell[?], did vpon or about the said 27

The personall answers of
John Lynes [?] late Commander
of the Shipp the Sampson
made to the pretensed
positions of an allegation
given against him on the
behalfe of Thomas Coul[?]ott
as Administrator of the goods
of John Coul[?]ott deceased.
To the first he answereth that hee beleeveth
that in or about the beginning of June
1648 and not before he the sayd John
Lynes [?] did goe forth as Commander of the
sayd Shipp the Sampson from this
port of London to Newfoundland and
did there safely arrive and take in his
lading of fish and from thence did goe
to the Brazeels [?] and there alsoe safely
arrived and delivered his lading of fish
or great parte thereof and was upon or
about the 22th, 1647 [?] seized upon with
his sayd Shipp and goods by authority of
and order from the King of Portugall as
hee beleeveth from which time hee was
divested of the power and Command thereof
and soe continued till about the 29th of

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8869
[page kind: text page; date as written: January 1652; document type: personal answer; legibility: 3]
January 1652 at or about which time hee was
pmitted by the sayd King of Portugall to take
the Command of the sayd Shippe upon
him againe And otherwise hee doth not
beleeve the sayd Article to be true in
any parte
To the Second third fowerth ffifth Sixth &
the rest of the Articles in the sayd allegacon
menconed hee answereth that hee doth not
beleeve that hee did hire the sayd John
Coulcrott [?] in the sayd Articles menconed or
that hee did goe in the sayd Shippe the
Sampson the sayd voyage or had any goods
or Chest of \[illegible]/ Sugar therein or that hee was
to pay him any wages or had any thing to
doe with him but doth beleeve that the
sayd John Coulcrott [?] in the sayd Articles
menconed was the very sayd voyage for wch
his wages are demaunded in this cause in
the service of the King of Portugall and did
goe out in his sayd service from Lisbone
and did arrive in the Brazeels [?] and after
=wards returned to Lixon [?] and there falling
into some quarrell was slaine and hee this
respondent and \[illegible]/ and severall other English men
then there resident did contribute towards
his buriall but hee never served this
[catchword] Re[illegible]dent [?]

HCA 13/125 IMG_115_05_8870
[page kind: text page; date as written: 16 Dec 1653; document type: personal answer; legibility: 3]
respondent as a Marriner nor was to haue
any wages or goods from him And other-
wise this rendent [?] doth not beleeue the —
sayd Articles or either of them to be true —
in any part saving that hee is a subiect
of this Commonwealth and that the sayd
wages and goods haue beene vnduly —
demanded from him and vniustly denyed —
and that hee hath beene vnlawfully troubled
and enforced to putt in bayle to the sayd =
pretended accon from which hee Con-
=ceiveth hee ought to be dismissed with
his Charges —
